### 2026-10-29 Customer read row-level authorization

Customer list/search already applied `CustomerCollectionPermissionFilter`, but the individual customer detail and current-balance endpoints queried a tenant-owned row directly. Both reads now apply the same allow/deny ABAC filter before returning customer fields or aggregating ledger entries; an inaccessible row returns 404. A shared customer-route endpoint filter now applies the same visibility check to every GET route carrying a customer `{id}`, covering nested addresses, statements, ledger and custom-field reads as well. List/detail parent-company IDs and names and subsidiary counts are now derived only from rows visible under the same filter, preventing relationship metadata from revealing hidden accounts. Existing authorization handling resolves customer type/active/id attributes for customer write routes and maps HTTP methods/routes to action-specific permissions. TenantAdmin behavior stays unchanged, and the shared forwarded caller token remains the authority for assistant balance reads. API Release build passed with 0 warnings/errors; `git diff --check` passed. No schema migration is needed. No tests were run and no remote deployment occurred; the dirty checkout includes broad unrelated API edits, so deployment was not attempted.

### Current backend follow-up — 2026-10-27

The foreign-trade country validator now accepts only assigned ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 codes; focused integration coverage rejects `DEU`, `AA`, and `ZZ` and passes the valid DE/TR lifecycle (1/1). The read-only electronic-document submission path now checks that submitted envelope identity/type/number/date match its tenant-owned posted ERP source and that document lines and positive quantities exist before queueing; 8 unit and 6 integration tests passed. The Release infrastructure build passed with 0 warnings/errors, Platform and Tenant report no pending model changes, and all package/manifest checksums verify. A clean-schema incremental rehearsal then applied the r3 idempotent baseline followed by each r4 incremental to three temporary local SQL Server 2025 databases. Migration counts/latest IDs matched the r4 manifest and `DBCC CHECKDB` found zero errors in all three; the temporary databases were dropped. This verifies clean-schema migration execution, not target backup restoration, existing-data compatibility, or production application. The possible historical migration-list read must still be resolved with authoritative DBA target-history evidence, followed by rehearsal on a checksum-verified target backup. Earlier local database applies are documented in the dated entries below. Focused Platform API integration tests for manufacturing and report endpoints passed 78/78 after source-link coverage was added; migration unit tests passed 3/3; API Release build passed with 0 warnings/errors; ERP Web production build passed. Manufacturing material cost reporting now uses a tenant-scoped source foreign key and leaves total cost/variance unavailable when historical material movements cannot be fully linked. The source now includes a role-level `erp.manufacturing.read` backfill for existing TenantUser memberships: it inserts only when no rule exists for that exact tenant/role/user/permission identity, preserving administrator-owned rules. Manufacturing screen errors are attributed to their originating read request so concurrent section refreshes do not overwrite unrelated error state; this source change has not received a fresh runtime acceptance pass. The local CodeQL 2.27.2 C# source scan completed all 59/59 standard queries against a 525-file `src` snapshot and reported one `cs/user-controlled-bypass` result at the AI sharing-consent assertion. Two reviews classify it as the per-request provider-sharing consent check, not an authentication or tenant-authorization bypass; this is local triage, not a hosted alert dismissal. GitHub-hosted CodeQL scan/alert refresh and production validation remain open.

2026-10-24 full local verification: `dotnet restore AIERP.slnx` and the Release solution build succeeded with 0 warnings/errors. The full solution test run passed 636 unit tests and 250 API integration tests; 11 SQL-connection-gated acceptance tests were skipped because no Docker SQL engine is available. The suite exposed a concurrent metrics-rendering race: sorting a live `ConcurrentDictionary` allowed LINQ to call `CopyTo` while metrics were being added. Rendering now snapshots entries through the dictionary's concurrency-safe enumerator before sorting. The race regression and previously failing assistant telemetry test passed, and the full unit and solution suites passed after the fix. Unhandled API errors are now logged with an opaque trace ID and return only a generic message plus that ID; ERP Web shows it to the user without exposing exception details. The focused handler test and full API suite passed. The AI settings panel was split into a lazy-loaded page to bring the ERP Web entry bundle below the 500 kB advisory; the production build now reports 491.53 kB for the entry bundle with no size warning, and the Development Studio build also passed. The exact r4 package passed clean-history checksum/count preflight (136/48/11) only; target history and schema/data compatibility remain unverified. Database-backed acceptance remains unexecuted.

2026-10-24 SQL-backed follow-up: after locating a local SQL Server 2025 Developer instance, the r3-to-r4 clean-schema incremental rehearsal passed for all three contexts; details and limits are recorded in `migration-r4-clean-schema-rehearsal.md`. The complete solution test run with `AIERP_REPORT_TEST_SQL_CONNECTION_STRING` passed 636 unit tests and 262 API integration tests; one legacy tenant-purge test remained skipped because it requires an explicitly designated disposable SQL instance. This exercised the previously gated SQL acceptance for fiscal-close serialization, license limits, negative-stock policy, return locations, payment instruments, TCMB rates, access control, and SQL reports. The SQL acceptance fixtures now run in a non-parallel collection because they temporarily set process-wide configuration; tenant-routing fixtures use unique canonical `AIERPTenant_<tenant-id>` database names. All generated test databases were removed. A GitHub CodeQL PR check still fails with 90 high `cs/path-injection` annotations; static review found custom containment/validation boundaries and no demonstrated remote traversal in the reviewed ReleaseDeployer and PluginHost paths, but the alerts have not been dismissed and the WorkspaceRunner review remains pending. The required CodeQL workflow itself passes; PR merge remains blocked by the separate alert check.

2026-10-24 finance acceptance follow-up: customer collection and supplier payment use the same tenant-row lock helper as fiscal-year close. Subsequent real HTTP SQL Server acceptance now races customer collection and supplier payment posting against close; each route serializes and later closed-period posting is rejected without ledger/journal/audit/cash side effects. Real payment-instrument collection is also raced against close; post-close portfolio operations are rejected without instrument-event, bank-movement, journal or audit side effects. Fixed-asset disposal now joins the existing acquisition SQL race coverage; both verify close serialization and a post-close rejection. These are representative route tests, not coverage of every posting route. Added a standalone supplier invoice draft regression test; it verifies no order, receipt, posted invoice, stock movement, supplier ledger, journal or payment row is created, including on an idempotent replay. This confirms draft-only capture, not accounting posting.

2026-10-24 CodeQL path-injection follow-up: read-only review of the 90 PR annotations found no remotely controlled traversal through the reviewed ReleaseDeployer, WorkspaceRunner, or PluginHost flows; their request-derived IDs are GUIDs or validated hashes and their path resolvers enforce canonical containment/reparse-point checks. It did identify a local filesystem time-of-check/time-of-use window if another actor can mutate the mounted storage concurrently; no current remote write primitive to that storage was found. The custom model pack is loaded by the hosted analysis but cannot affect `cs/path-injection`: CodeQL 2.27.3's `TaintedPathConfig` only consumes its built-in `Sanitizer` hierarchy and does not consume external `barrierModel` or `barrierGuardModel` rows. Corrected the pack's guard access paths to target validated `out` parameters, but this does not resolve hosted alerts. No alert was suppressed or dismissed; the PR CodeQL gate remains failed pending a real vulnerability fix or authoritative scan-baseline resolution.

2026-10-22 continuation: separated tenant databases are mandatory in Platform API, AI Service and Release Deployer; shared runtime fallback has been removed. `DATABASE_SEPARATION_ENABLED=false` is only a Docker migration-source marker and does not alter current Compose runtime mode. On a brand-new Platform database created in the same bootstrap run, initial tenant/Studio provisioning can proceed without a prior backup because the source had no pre-existing data; first-time copies from an existing Platform database still require a recent checksum-verified backup. Authenticated ERP or Studio data requests without a selected tenant now return 403 instead of reaching `TenantDbContext`/`AIStudioDbContext` without a routed connection; unprovisioned selected tenants remain 503. Tenant-routing tests passed 9/9, API and AI Service builds passed with zero warnings/errors, and the Linux deployment preflight plus Docker Compose config checks passed. The earlier no-apply state was superseded on 2026-10-22 by the r2 rehearsal and local Docker apply recorded below.

## Verification snapshot

- Current local Compose recovery and regression (2026-10-20): Docker Desktop restarted successfully. The Platform API had been left in `created` state, so the Studio nginx container could not resolve its `platform-api` upstream and repeatedly restarted. Starting `platform-api` brought Studio and ERP Web healthy; ERP Web `/`, Studio `/`, Platform API `/health`, and AI Service `/health` all returned HTTP 200. The full solution suite passed 67 API integration and 457 unit tests (524 total, 0 failures/skips); Release solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors; ERP Web and Development Studio production builds passed. A shared security-header middleware is now wired to Platform API, AI Service, PluginHost, IntegrationHost, ReleaseDeployer, and WorkspaceRunner; the latter three images were rebuilt and recreated, and their internal `/health` responses each returned HTTP 200 with `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. The authenticated TenantAdmin OpenAI model-list request accepted the saved key and returned 82 models, including the configured `gpt-6-luna`. After the user asked to enable data sharing, the consent control was enabled and a synthetic-only AI ERP Assistant chat (`2 + 3`) returned `5`; the prompt contained no ERP records. This confirms a live chat completion, but not semantic quality on ERP questions. No database migration was required. `git diff --check` passed.
- Live AI chat smoke (2026-10-20): with the user's data-sharing request reflected in the ERP Assistant consent control, sent two synthetic prompts to the configured OpenAI model `gpt-6-luna`; the interface returned the expected results `5` and `17`. A third, explicitly authorized read-only `list_warehouses` request returned successfully; sanitized AI Service telemetry recorded one successful ERP tool call. Three assistant requests returned HTTP 200. No warehouse names, IDs, prompt text, or response values were saved in `docs/ai-live-chat-smoke-20261020.json`. This verifies one live ERP read path, not semantic quality across the report catalog; the versioned representative answer-quality evaluation remains open.
- Hosted CodeQL/PR recheck (2026-10-20): GitHub CLI authentication is active. PR #1 (`codex/phase-completion-clean` → `master`) is OPEN and MERGEABLE; .NET tests, ERP Web and Development Studio builds, C# and JavaScript/TypeScript CodeQL analyses, and the CodeQL gate all pass on head `b0e201c54bdf0391bccef1c541784fae12b1706f`. - WorkspaceSandbox previous-image scan (2026-10-20; superseded by the final remediated digest entry below): Docker Scout rescanned the running image `sha256:5168358c91320d7d484a7c955d6f3941f603d4c70239c2569fe1eec2f6540580`, indexing 1,143 packages and reporting 27 records (4 HIGH, 16 MEDIUM, 5 LOW, 2 unspecified) across six packages. The local image and containers were not modified. Full SARIF evidence is `docs/docker-scout-workspace-sandbox-20261020.sarif.json` and disposition is in `docs/sandbox-image-risk-review.md`.

- AI assistant tool breadth and chaining (2026-10-19): expanded the read-only report tool from 13 to all 27 report catalog sources using the existing tenant-authorized Reports API, including TenantAdmin-only workflow history (the API catalog and run endpoint enforce that role). Added a tenant-authorized report-schema lookup so the model sees exact valid field names, and enabled at most three sequential tool calls per answer so a customer search can be followed by a balance lookup or a schema lookup by a report query. The assistant is instructed not to present its bounded 10-row page as the full dataset or combine currencies. Focused assistant orchestration/provider tests passed 38/38; the AI Service image rebuilt with a 67 KB Docker context, was recreated without dependent services, and its `/health` endpoint returned HTTP 200. Live-model semantic quality and account-specific authorized acceptance remain unverified.
- Studio knowledge SQL translation recovery (2026-10-19): server logs identified EF Core's `EmptyProjectionMember` exception when filtering/projecting grouped latest knowledge-document entities. SQL Server `ToQueryString()` regression test passed; the AI Service rebuilt and is healthy, and fresh authenticated smoke requests to the knowledge and conversation-message endpoints returned HTTP 200. No chat content was read into tool output or sent to an AI provider. The browser's prior 401 responses require a fresh sign-in; live chat POST remains unverified.

- Manufacturing slice (2026-10-17): tenant-scoped bill-of-materials and production-order APIs support recipe component/scrap snapshots, planned/released/completed/cancelled order states, serialized SQL Server completion, reservation-aware component availability, FEFO lot and serial component allocation, atomic issues/receipts, weighted-average material costing and audit events. Tracked finished goods accept operator-supplied lot/expiry or unique serial allocations at completion, and ERP Web follows the product tracking mode. The MRP endpoint/screen combine open sales backlog (excluding posted deliveries and warehouse reservations), dated extra demand, warehouse stock, dated open production and purchase balances, then recursively explode active BOMs with cycle/depth/row bounds and make/buy recommendations. Work centers define daily minute capacity and hourly labor/overhead rates; BOM routes capture sequence/setup/run time, production orders snapshot those rates and planned costs, a finite-capacity allocator reserves daily minutes in routing order, and release requires a complete schedule. Released operations start and complete in sequence; operators record actual setup/run minutes, and completion calculates operation labor/overhead from the snapshotted rates and rolls actual operation cost into finished-goods unit cost. Integration tests cover multi-level netting, sales demand, warehouse reservations and tenant isolation; lot/serial tracking; routing snapshots, multi-day capacity, release gating, actual operation start/completion, actual-cost rollup and blocked order completion; plus costing and tenant isolation. Full `dotnet build AIERP.slnx --no-restore` and ERP Web production build passed; Web reports the existing >500 KiB bundle warning. Actual-operation migrations `20261017122605_AddManufacturingOperationActuals` and `20261017122651_AddTenantManufacturingOperationActuals` were generated. Time-phased MRP now uses dated extra demands and audited sales-order required delivery dates, allocates only supply available by each need date, and subtracts procurement/manufacturing lead times from recommended order/start dates. The MRP and sales-date integration tests passed; full solution suite passed 260 unit and 31 API integration tests. PlatformDbContext and TenantDbContext report no pending model changes. MRP migrations `20261017123808_AddTimePhasedMrp`, `20261017123905_AddTenantTimePhasedMrp`, sales-date migrations `20261017124845_AddSalesOrderRequiredDeliveryDate` and `20261017124947_AddTenantSalesOrderRequiredDeliveryDate` were generated. Migrations were not applied because the user chose to preserve Docker data and defer recovery. Remaining manufacturing gaps include sector-specific workflows, SQL Server-backed migrations and wider end-to-end acceptance coverage.
- CRM opportunity slice (2026-10-17): added tenant-scoped opportunity list/create/update/stage APIs, valid sales-pipeline transitions, same-tenant customer references, audit events, and the ERP CRM Fırsatları screen. Generated PlatformDbContext and TenantDbContext migrations; the UI production build passed and CRM API integration tests passed 2/2. API build passed with 0 errors (one transient file-copy retry warning caused by the concurrently running test build). At this earlier snapshot migrations were deferred; subsequent local Compose entries record the applied CRM schema migrations and live SQL-backed acceptance.
- Time-phased MRP slice (2026-10-17): stock cards now store bounded procurement/manufacturing lead-time days; purchase orders can store a tenant-scoped target warehouse and expected receipt date, and stock/purchase-order screens expose those settings. The MRP accepts dated extra demand and audited sales-order required delivery dates, allocates stock plus only dated/warehouse-matched PO and production supply available by each need date, explodes manufacturing component needs at the suggested start date, and returns required-by and recommended order/start dates. Tests verified that a late PO is not netted early, an eligible dated PO is netted on time, lead time moves the recommended order date, and a sales-order required date drives MRP; update API tenant isolation passed. Full solution suite passed (260 unit, 31 API integration); solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors, ERP Web production build passed with the existing >500 KiB chunk warning, and both EF contexts report no pending model changes. Generated migrations: PlatformDbContext `20261017123808_AddTimePhasedMrp` and `20261017124845_AddSalesOrderRequiredDeliveryDate`; TenantDbContext `20261017123905_AddTenantTimePhasedMrp` and `20261017124947_AddTenantSalesOrderRequiredDeliveryDate`. Legacy POs without expected date or target warehouse are excluded from time-phased incoming supply; sales orders without a required-by date are treated as due on the plan date. Migrations remain unapplied while Docker data recovery is deferred. Remaining MRP work: verify migrations and conversion transactions against SQL Server, then extend sector-specific and end-to-end workflows.

- Phase 3 purchase-request report follow-up (2026-10-17): added `purchaseRequests` to the fixed catalog with requester user ID, status, request/needed-by/review dates and description fields; typed filters, stable sorting/paging, count grouping, bounded formula-safe CSV and audit events. Report and export queries reuse the purchasing endpoint's status/owner ABAC filter in addition to tenant scoping. The report reads requester IDs from tenant data and does not depend on a cross-database membership/user join. No schema migration is needed. End-to-end UI coverage remains open.

Phase 3 includes a tenant-scoped custom form workflow in the ERP UI and API. TenantAdmins can create drafts, edit typed fields, publish versions, inspect history, restore prior revisions, create a new draft from a published form, and approve or reject submissions with a reason. Tenant users can fill published forms and review their own submissions. The API validates field codes, types, required values, declared choices, lengths and payload size; each submission stores the exact published schema and version. PlatformDbContext and TenantDbContext migrations include these entities so both shared and separated tenant database deployments can apply them.

The reusable workflow engine supports tenant-admin-authored, versioned definitions with up to 20 sequential role approval steps, publishing and cloning drafts. Tenant users can start a workflow against a descriptive business record; every instance stores the published steps and bounded context snapshot. Approvals are assigned by tenant role, requesters cannot self-approve, decisions are audited, and SQL rowversion prevents concurrent advancement. Tenant business rules support bounded typed conditions (text, decimal, date, and empty checks) for `purchase-request.submitted` and `sales-quote.accepted`. A tenant admin can publish one active rule per event and route matching records to a published approval workflow. Evaluation, workflow creation, and the source business transition share one database transaction. For purchase requests, manual approval is blocked while the workflow is pending and the terminal decision updates the request; for accepted sales quotes, order conversion is blocked while pending, approval preserves Accepted status, and rejection sets Rejected. Rule evaluations are retained for review. The report builder currently supports tenant-scoped customer-account, sales-invoice, purchase-order, purchase-request, and stock-card reports, typed AND filters, stable sorting, pagination, one-to-three-field SQL grouping, count and source-specific aggregates, null-aware group-to-detail drill-down through typed tenant-scoped filters, and formula-safe CSV export capped at 10,000 matching or grouped rows; sales-invoice and purchase-order monetary aggregates require currency grouping or an exact single-currency filter. Stock-card reports expose minimum-stock configuration plus on-hand, active unfulfilled reservations, and available quantities across tenant warehouses. Tenant admins can save/update/delete report definitions and list views; list views are stored as a distinct kind, cannot group, and reuse the same bounded tenant-scoped query engine. All executions and mutations are audited. Saved reports can be pinned, reordered, previewed and removed as tenant dashboard widgets; widget mutations and saved-report references are tenant-scoped and audited. Additional report sources, additional ERP event adapters, and end-to-end workflow coverage remain incomplete. PlatformDbContext and TenantDbContext migrations include these tables; shared local Compose Platform migrations are applied, while separate TenantDbContext migrations are generated for tenant database provisioning.

- Phase 3 business-rule follow-up (2026-10-17): added the `sales-quote.accepted` event with a server-provided condition catalog and typed quote facts, while retaining `purchase-request.submitted` as the backward-compatible default. Accepted quotes can now start the tenant's published approval workflow; order conversion is blocked while the review is pending, approval leaves the quote accepted, and rejection marks it rejected. The ERP business-rule screen loads event and field options from the API. Integration coverage checks API configuration/publishing, unsupported condition fields, matching and nonmatching quotes, the conversion gate, and both decisions. Latest verification: solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors, 212 unit tests passed, 11 API integration tests passed, ERP Web production build passed, and local Compose reports the Platform API healthy; the new business-rule catalog rejects anonymous access with HTTP 401. Vite reports the existing main bundle above 500 kB. No schema migration was required. Remaining event adapters and full browser end-to-end coverage are open.

- Phase 8 declarative module UI follow-up (2026-10-17): signed menu entries can now declare a bounded form input and/or typed result-table columns. `ModuleManifestValidator` limits fields/options and rejects unsupported input modes, field types, duplicate field codes, and empty table schemas. ERP Web renders native form controls and text-only tables; it falls back to JSON when `view` is absent or a table response has no rows. Existing manifests remain compatible through an optional `view` contract. SDK docs contain the contract example. Solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors, 215 unit tests and 11 API integration tests passed, ERP Web production build passed, and local Compose API/PluginHost report healthy; anonymous module catalog access returns 401. Vite still reports the >500 kB main bundle advisory. No migration was required. Capability-mediated ERP data access, adapter execution and lifecycle observability remain open.

- Phase 8 host-mediated data follow-up (2026-10-17): module manifests can declare the fixed `customers.search` capability and reference an independent module permission. The API validates the signed manifest, authorizes the menu action and capability separately through tenant ABAC, executes a bounded tenant-scoped read against `TenantDbContext`, and injects only customer ID/code/name/currency into the WASM input. Search terms and result counts are bounded; unknown query fields/capabilities are rejected; denied reads are audited without recording query/result content. Modules retain no direct database, network or host API access. Optional-null manifest fields are omitted from canonical signed JSON so existing package signatures remain valid. Solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors, 216 unit tests and 14 API integration tests passed. Local Compose rebuilt Platform API and PluginHost; both report healthy. Anonymous module catalog and invocation requests return HTTP 401. No database migration was needed. Additional capability operations and real adapter execution remain open.

- Phase 8 inventory capability follow-up (2026-10-17): added `inventory.availability.search` to the fixed module capability catalog with an independent signed-manifest permission. Each signed menu view can request only the capabilities explicitly bound to it; the ERP form derives the bounded query and the API enforces that binding as well as tenant ABAC. InMemory integration coverage verifies 20 received − 5 issued = 15 on hand, 4 reserved − 1 fulfilled = 3 reserved, 12 available, cross-tenant exclusion, and ABAC denial. Full-solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors; 217 unit tests and 15 API integration tests passed; ERP Web production build passed (Vite still reports the >500 kB main-bundle advisory). Local Compose API and PluginHost are healthy; anonymous invoke requests return 401. Warehouse-specific selection and other operations remain open.

- Phase 8 customer balance follow-up (2026-10-17): added fixed `customers.balance.read`, requiring a signed module declaration, a capability-bound menu form with a required `customer-code` field, the independent module permission, and tenant ABAC. Integration coverage verifies USD debit/credit arithmetic, risk limit, record-level ABAC denial, and cross-tenant hiding. Verification: full solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors; 218 unit tests and 16 API integration tests passed; ERP Web production build passed (Vite reports the existing >500 kB main-bundle advisory); Compose config validation passed; rebuilt local Platform API and PluginHost are healthy, `/health` returns 200, and anonymous invocation returns 401. - Phase 8 Integration SDK resilience follow-up (2026-10-17): added `IntegrationAdapterExecutor` with bounded policy validation, cancellation/deadline handling, retry of retryable outcomes and transport/time-out exceptions, stable idempotency context across attempts, allowlisted error codes, sanitized adapter exceptions, and bounded result metadata. Unit coverage verifies retry success, exact attempt cap, stable idempotency key, exception-message redaction and expired-deadline rejection. Full solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors; 219 unit tests and 17 API integration tests passed. A durable queue/idempotency implementation, isolated integration host, production secret resolver, mappings and vendor-specific live adapters were still open at that point.

- Phase 8 isolated integration and durable queue follow-up (2026-10-17): API requests now enter PlatformDB `IntegrationOperations` before acknowledgement; request and result JSON are AES-GCM encrypted with tenant/operation/purpose binding. Client keys are hashed at rest and tenant/integration unique across the 30-day retention window; reusing a key with a different payload is rejected. `POST /api/integrations/{integrationCode}/execute` returns an operation resource, and `GET /api/integrations/operations/{operationId}` requires read permission and limits visibility to its tenant and requesting user. An API BackgroundService claims queue rows using SQL Server `UPDLOCK/READPAST`, retries delivery at most three times with one tenant-namespaced provider key, audits outcomes, and removes terminal records after 30 days. Uncertain external effects end in `Indeterminate` for provider reconciliation. The separate IntegrationHost remains private, non-root, read-only-root, no-capability, resource-limited, and without Docker socket or published port; it permits bounded HTTPS JSON requests to exactly allowlisted hosts and reads tenant secrets from a read-only mount. Migration `20261017052400_AddIntegrationOperationQueue` was applied to local SQL Server. `dotnet test AIERP.slnx` passed: 223 unit tests and 18 API integration tests. Live Compose smoke with the authenticated bootstrap TenantAdmin returned 202, the SQL-backed worker completed the request as `Failed/integration_not_configured` with one attempt (there are zero configured adapters), retrying the same key/payload returned 200 with the same operation ID, and changing the payload under that key returned 409. Platform API and IntegrationHost health returned 200; anonymous API and host execution returned 401. No vendor endpoint/credential/mapping has been live-tested. Delivery is at least once; provider-side idempotency is still required to avoid duplicate side effects.

The AI UI Designer accepts a bounded natural-language screen brief after explicit AI-sharing consent and returns a schema-validated declarative plan. Studio renders a safe visual preview and deterministic React/TypeScript; generated code is not executed, existing source is not sent, and saving to a new workspace file requires separate explicit approval. The dedicated `studio.ai-design` permission is included in PlatformDbContext; migration `20261016214200_AddAiUiDesignerPermission` was applied by local Compose. Human code-review threads are stored separately from AI findings, scope every query by tenant/project/workspace, anchor comments to commit and line, and support replies plus resolve/reopen with SQL rowversion concurrency. Published GitHub PR identity is persisted on its release candidate. Maintainers can request GitHub reviewers, submit COMMENT/APPROVE/REQUEST_CHANGES reviews (candidate creators cannot self-approve), and explicitly sync comments both ways; incoming comments retain repository/PR/comment identities and GitHub usernames, deduplicate on repeat sync, and are only anchored when GitHub supplies a safe source path, line and commit. Both shared and separated AI Studio database migrations are generated; shared Platform migrations through `20261016230504_AddStudioGitHubCommentRepositoryPlatform` are applied by local Compose. No live GitHub posting or authenticated GitHub App sync was exercised. Remaining work includes broader PR lifecycle automation and authenticated GitHub integration verification.

Phase 8 is not complete: signed WASM modules can be validated and invoked in an isolated runtime; each menu action is bound to its declared module permission and an ABAC policy, and optional signed view metadata renders bounded forms and result tables. The API currently injects fixed customer search/balance and stock availability capabilities after independent module permission and tenant ABAC checks; modules still have no direct database/network/host access. IntegrationHost now has a bounded HTTPS adapter path and a persisted encrypted queue/idempotency ledger, but provider-specific mappings/connectors, additional business-data capabilities, and broader lifecycle/runtime health observability remain open. The queue was exercised against a live SQL-backed authenticated request with no provider configured; vendor outcomes and cross-process recovery still need deployment verification. Phase 9 is incomplete: local Compose is single-host, automatic failover is unavailable, and representative-data production recovery remains unverified. Platform API now runs with Production error handling in Compose; a malformed authenticated request returned 400 without echoing its bearer token. A local SQL backup/restore drill and read-path load/security/dependency checks are now evidenced, while SAST, container scanning, penetration testing, broader load scenarios, and HA failover remain open.


Phase 4 includes consent-gated, versioned source indexing: Contributors explicitly select up to 50 supported files; API enforces sensitive path/content checks, 128 KiB per file and 512 KiB total bounds, records content SHA-256 and the working-tree version, and stores source text and optional embeddings in the AI Studio data model (or shared Platform schema, with a separate AIStudio schema available for separated deployments). Reads, indexing, deletion and retrieval are tenant/project/workspace scoped. On indexing, OpenAI embeddings are created only after explicit confirmation; source is split into deterministic line-addressable chunks, bounded to 2,800 characters and sent in batches. On chat, the user separately opts in for that message; a query embedding ranks only latest per-path snapshots with a minimum cosine threshold, then returns at most five files and 10,000 characters with paths, lines and content hashes. Source content remains untrusted instruction data. NVIDIA or unavailable embeddings fall back to keyword retrieval. A daily retention worker deletes snapshots older than `STUDIO_KNOWLEDGE_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 180 days; allowed range 30–3650), including latest snapshots, which must be explicitly re-indexed with sharing consent. The index still does not automatically ingest a repository or ERP records. Approved development history is now a manually selected source: only independently approved release candidates are listed, and the request text and independent review note are indexed with the candidate commit after explicit AI-sharing confirmation. Secret scanning, 64 KiB per history record and 512 KiB aggregate bounds apply; hashes deduplicate unchanged records, and normal tenant/project/workspace retention and retrieval rules apply. API contracts, schema, rules and wider end-to-end tests remain open; automatic index refresh is not implemented.

- Phase 8 integration mapping follow-up (2026-10-17): IntegrationHost applies operator-defined JSON field mappings before outbound calls; configured mappings send only allowlisted fields, support bounded nested object paths and required-field validation, and reject invalid/overlapping targets before network access. With no mappings the existing payload is passed through unchanged. Integration SDK unit coverage verifies nested mapping, omission of unmapped fields, required/invalid/conflicting paths and backward compatibility; an IntegrationDispatcher HTTP-capture test verifies the provider receives only mapped data. Full solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors; full test run passed 227 unit tests and 18 API integration tests. Local Compose IntegrationHost was rebuilt and is healthy; `/health` returns `configuredAdapters: 0`, so no external vendor is claimed as configured or verified. No database migration was required. Provider-specific response mappings/connectors and live vendor verification remain open.

- Phase 9 load baseline follow-up (2026-10-17): added `tests/performance/Invoke-PlatformLoadTest.ps1`, which authenticates without logging/storing credentials, warms the same HTTP connection pool, then measures only tenant-authorized read-only `GET /api/dashboard` requests. Default gate: p95 ≤ 1,000 ms and error rate ≤ 1%. Local Compose steady-state run passed at 25 concurrency (300/300 HTTP 200, p95 36 ms, 0% errors, 1,086.96 req/s); stress run passed at 100 concurrency (1,000/1,000 HTTP 200, p95 688 ms, 0% errors, 667.11 req/s). Aggregates are saved in `docs/performance-baseline.json` and `docs/performance-stress.json`; an initial cold-client-pool diagnostic (p95 2,185 ms) is retained separately and excluded from steady-state comparison. These are single-machine, low-data local results, not production capacity claims. API behavior/schema did not change; no migration was needed.

The local Compose run passed 6/6 checks: API ready, anonymous tenant-dashboard and integration-execution requests denied with 401, authenticated tenant selection succeeds, invalid authenticated report query returns 400, and the bearer token is absent from that error body and response headers. `docs/security-smoke.json` stores statuses/assertions only. This smoke is not a SAST/dependency scanner, penetration test, or production assessment; those and HA/recovery drills remain open. No database migration was needed.

- Phase 9 dependency audit follow-up (2026-10-17): added `tests/security/Invoke-DependencyAudit.ps1` to scan `AIERP.slnx` including transitive NuGet packages and run npm advisories for both frontends. The current advisory scan passed with 0 findings across all three scopes; output is saved in `docs/dependency-audit.json`. No code/dependency changes or database migration were required.

- Phase 9 SQL recovery drill (2026-10-17): added `tests/recovery/Invoke-LocalSqlRestoreDrill.ps1`. On local Compose, it created a `COPY_ONLY` backup of `AIERPPlatform`, passed `RESTORE VERIFYONLY WITH CHECKSUM`, restored into a unique temporary database, passed `DBCC CHECKDB`, and confirmed 134 user tables in both databases. The source and restored row totals differed by two because the live source changed during the backup; row totals are reported as diagnostics, not compared as a same-instant invariant. The script removed both temporary database and backup file. The drill exposed that the SQL backup volume was root-owned and not writable by SQL Server; a one-shot Compose initializer now sets that volume root to UID 10001 and mode 0775 before SQL Server starts, in the main stack and generated staging/production stacks. No application database migration was needed. Automated failover and production-target recovery remain open.

- Phase 9 local SQL restart recovery drill (2026-10-18): added `tests/recovery/Invoke-LocalSqlRestartDrill.ps1`. On the separated local Compose stack, it first required the active Studio, Platform API, and AI Service HTTP endpoints to return 200; it then stopped SQL Server with a 30-second graceful timeout and restarted the existing container without touching volumes or applying migrations. The run passed: SQL container healthy, all 10 previously running containers recovered (health checks healthy where defined), Platform + Studio + tenant databases online, and all three HTTP endpoints returned 200. Metadata-only evidence is stored in `docs/sql-restart-recovery-drill.json`. This proves single-node service recovery after a planned restart; automated failover, production-target recovery and HA remain open.

- Phase 5/6 PR lifecycle follow-up (2026-10-17): AI Service accepts GitHub `pull_request` webhook events only after fixed-time HMAC-SHA256 verification against `GitHub:WebhookSecret`; request bodies are capped at 1 MiB and the unauthenticated endpoint is rate-limited. Open/reopened/synchronize/closed actions update linked candidates in the shared or separated AI Studio database. The candidate stores GitHub `updated_at`, so stale/replayed events are ignored and a merged state cannot be reverted; actual state changes create tenant-scoped audit events. Other signed actions are acknowledged without mutation. Compose wires `GITHUB_APP_WEBHOOK_SECRET`; configure a random secret of at least 32 UTF-8 bytes in the secret store and the matching GitHub App webhook settings. Unit tests cover valid/tampered signatures, weak secrets, merged-state parsing, unsupported actions and stale/duplicate state updates. Generated PlatformDbContext migration `20261017133531_AddGitHubPullRequestUpdatedAtPlatform` and AIStudioDbContext migration `20261017133602_AddGitHubPullRequestUpdatedAtAIStudio`; both contexts report no pending model changes. Full solution build passed with 0 warnings/errors; full suite passed (263 unit, 35 API integration). Migrations are not applied while Docker data recovery is deferred. Authenticated delivery from an actual GitHub App remains unverified; broader PR lifecycle automation is still open.

- Phase 4 automatic knowledge refresh (2026-10-17): contributors can separately authorize up to 50 explicitly selected workspace files for refresh immediately before opted-in AI chat retrieval. The chat request still requires per-message AI-sharing confirmation; refresh is limited to the current working tree, tenant/project/workspace scope, 128 KiB per file and 512 KiB total. Missing or secret-bearing files create an empty latest snapshot to prevent stale content from resurfacing and are removed from the refresh allowlist. Contributors can inspect and revoke the allowlist; consent rows expire with knowledge retention. Manual deletion removes every snapshot and withdraws that path from the refresh allowlist. Platform and AIStudio migrations `20261017134602_AddStudioKnowledgeRefreshConsentPlatform` and `20261017134712_AddStudioKnowledgeRefreshConsentAIStudio` were generated and not applied while Docker data recovery is deferred. `dotnet build AIERP.slnx --no-restore` passed with 0 warnings/errors, Studio `npm run build` passed, and the full solution test suite passed (267 unit, 35 API integration), including four new automatic-refresh tests for consent scoping, removed-source tombstones, fail-closed Runner errors, and the no-consent path. Database-backed refresh/revocation, migration application and live Runner/OpenAI refresh remain unverified.

- Phase 5 AI Database Engineer (2026-10-17): added a workspace-only database engineering agent. Contributors select 1–10 safe SQL/ORM/model files; only those paths are read through Runner, with secret scanning and 32 KiB/file, 128 KiB total bounds. Per-request consent gates sending source to OpenAI. Structured output includes read-only SQL, descriptive migration design, index/performance recommendations and integrity checks. A server validator rejects DDL/DML, execution tokens, comments, multi-statement SQL and `SELECT INTO`; the service never connects to the application database or applies a proposal. Plans are transient; audit events store request/result metadata without source contents. `dotnet build AIERP.slnx --no-restore` passed with 0 warnings/errors, Development Studio `npm run build` passed, and full tests passed (279 unit, 35 API integration), including SQL safety and mocked provider contract coverage. No migration is required. Live OpenAI and authenticated workspace Runner behavior remain unverified.

- Phase 4 ERP assistant tool expansion (2026-10-17): added typed, read-only customer/supplier account search and customer-balance lookup tools to the consent-gated OpenAI Responses loop. Multi-turn history allows a user to search an account and then request its balance without persisting chat records. Mocked OpenAI tool-loop tests passed with the provider suite (20/20); live provider execution and authenticated per-role authorization remain unverified. No migration is required.

- Phase 4 bounded report tool (2026-10-17): the consent-gated ERP assistant can now run tenant-scoped operational detail reports through the existing authorized report API. Tool arguments are bounded to catalog sources, 1–10 columns, five typed filters, and a ten-row first page; results retain only selected columns, truncate text cells to 256 characters, and cap the serialized payload at 16,000 characters. It does not save reports, group monetary data, or bypass the caller's bearer authorization. Provider tool-loop tests passed 21/21; the report-payload bound test passed 1/1; AI service builds passed with zero warnings/errors. No migration is required. Live OpenAI execution, SQL Server report translation, and per-role authenticated acceptance remain unverified.

- Phase 3 financial transfer report: added tenant-scoped source/target account reporting with source and destination currencies/amounts, exchange rate, safe filters, all-field sorting, paging, audited bounded CSV, and cross-tenant exclusion. - Phase 3 general ledger report: added tenant-scoped journal-line reporting with ledger-account names, line debit/credit, entry-wide totals and balance status, safe filters, all-field sorting, paging, audited bounded CSV, and cross-tenant isolation even when tenants reuse the same account code. Integration acceptance passed 1/1. No migration was needed.
- Phase 4 provider failure handling: HTTP 429 now maps to a sanitized rate-limit-or-quota-unknown failure, without reading the response body or retrying automatically. The versioned offline orchestration evaluation passed 12/12 cases, including this classification; zero external requests were made and semantic quality is not scored by the mock. Live evaluation remains open because the only live synthetic attempt returned HTTP 429 and its cause is unknown.
- Phase 5 PR publish race recovery: after GitHub rejects concurrent duplicate PR creation with HTTP 422, the service re-queries the exact owner/head/base and reuses the matching open PR. Focused tests passed 2/2. No live GitHub write was made.
- Phase 6 Go diagnostics: common compiler messages including `undefined:` and `declared and not used` now enter source-failure triage rather than the unknown category. Focused tests passed 16/16.
- Phase 9 WorkspaceSandbox risk: the current digest was scanned with Docker Scout v1.26.0; 1,158 packages were indexed and 34 vulnerability records found. Six applicable HIGH findings remain in binutils, libxml2 and libssh2, all reported without fixed versions. Release Deployer package verification now hashes and parses the same open package handle and streams each extracted file once into a temporary file while hashing its contents before atomic placement. ReleaseDeployer orchestration acceptance passed 2/2. Full solution regression after these changes passed 61 API integration plus 401 unit tests (462 total, 0 failed/skipped); Release build passed with 0 warnings/errors. `git diff --check` reported no whitespace errors. CodeQL alerts remain against the older published SHA and were not dismissed; hosted rescan still requires publishing these changes.
- Phase 3/6/4/8 acceptance follow-up (2026-10-19): CSV export now detects spreadsheet formula markers after leading spaces or tabs; the regression failed before the fix and passed afterward. Phase 6 webhook coverage proves an older delayed `closed` snapshot with a changed head SHA cannot regress newer PR state, tested commit, or release eligibility and emits no audit event. The ERP assistant now caps warehouse tool results at 100 rows, 128 characters per text field and 16,000 serialized characters; the offline bound regression passed 1/1. Stripe PaymentIntent responses now fail closed for unknown status strings; fake-HTTP adapter tests passed 4/4. The focused report test passed 1/1 and webhook lifecycle tests passed 2/2. CodeQL follow-up found no additional independently exploitable path-injection case in the inspected IntegrationHost secret-file and WorkspaceSandbox extraction flows; remaining alerts still require exact sink triage and a hosted scan. Broader report datasets/saved-report UX, complete PR automation, authenticated GitHub App E2E, live-provider semantic evaluation, and Stripe/PayPal sandbox/reconciliation remain open.

The Windows-specific regression passed 1/1 and the ReleaseDeployer Release build passed with 0 warnings/errors. Full-solution regression including the alias fix and all prior follow-ups passed 62 API integration and 404 unit tests (466 total, 0 failed/skipped); after the Phase 6 diagnostic fixtures, the latest total is 62 API integration plus 410 unit tests (472 total, 0 failed/skipped). - 2026-10-24 tenant license storage enforcement: each routed tenant SQL database now measures SQL Server `ROWS` data-page allocation before and after EF Core SaveChanges and before an interceptor-owned transaction commits. At/over the licensed limit, mutations are rejected and rolled back when they increase measured page usage; reads work, and deletions (including their required audit rows) are accepted only when measured page usage does not increase. An unverifiable meter/license denies the write. The TCMB worker creates a per-tenant scope so its quota session lock is released between firms. API build passed with 0 warnings/errors; all three SQL Server acceptance tests passed against temporary databases on Docker SQL Server 2022, covering below/at/over limit, reads, deletion at/over limit, rollback for interceptor-owned and caller-owned transactions, and scope lock release. No schema migration was needed or applied. Concurrent license downgrade/write serialization and all bypass coverage remain open; enforcement currently covers EF Core SaveChanges additions/modifications and deletions only when page usage does not increase.
